   140	              failedRemovals.map((result) => `${result.agent}: ${result.error || 'removal failed'}`).join(', ') ||
   141	              t('settings.mcpRemoveFailed')
   142	          );
   143	        }
   144	        return removeResponse.data?.results ?? [];
   145	      } else {
   146	        throw new Error(agentsResponse.msg || t('settings.mcpSyncFailedNoAgents'));
   147	      }
   148	    },
   149	    [message, t, handleMcpOperationResult]
   150	  );
   151	
   152	  // Sync MCP config to agents
   153	  const syncMcpToAgents = useCallback(
   154	    async (server: IMcpServer, skipRecheck = false) => {
   155	      const agentsResponse = await acpConversation.getAvailableAgents.invoke();
   156	      if (agentsResponse.success && agentsResponse.data) {
   157	        // Filter agents by transport type support to show accurate count
   158	        const compatibleCount = agentsResponse.data.filter((a) =>
   159	          a.supportedTransports?.includes(server.transport.type)
   160	        ).length;
   161	
   162	        // Show sync-started message (via queue)
   163	        await globalMessageQueue.add(() => {
   164	          message.info({ content: t('settings.mcpSyncStarted', { count: compatibleCount }), icon: progressIcon() });
   165	        });
   166	
   167	        // Desktop -> Electron IPC; hosted WebUI -> token-authed + CSRF'd write-only
   168	        // HTTP route (the server is resolved server-side by id over HTTP).
   169	        const syncResponse = isElectronDesktop()
   170	          ? await mcpService.syncMcpToAgents.invoke({
   171	              mcpServers: [server],
   172	              agents: agentsResponse.data,
   173	            })
   174	          : await syncMcpToAgentsHttp(server.id);
   175	
   176	        await handleMcpOperationResult(syncResponse, 'sync', undefined, skipRecheck);
   177	        const publicationResults = syncResponse.data?.results ?? [];
   178	        const failedPublications = publicationResults.filter((result) => !result.success);
   179	        if (!syncResponse.success || publicationResults.length === 0 || failedPublications.length > 0) {
   180	          throw new Error(syncResponse.msg || t('settings.mcpSyncFailedNoAgents'));
   181	        }
   182	        return syncResponse.data?.results ?? [];
   183	      } else {
   184	        // Fix: Handle case when no agents are available, show user-friendly error message
   185	        console.error('[useMcpOperations] Failed to get available agents:', agentsResponse.msg);
   186	        await globalMessageQueue.add(() => {
   187	          message.error(t('settings.mcpSyncFailedNoAgents'));
   188	        });
   189	        throw new Error(agentsResponse.msg || t('settings.mcpSyncFailedNoAgents'));
   190	      }
   191	    },
   192	    [message, t, handleMcpOperationResult]
   193	  );
   194	
   195	  return {
   196	    syncMcpToAgents,
   197	    removeMcpFromAgents,
   198	    handleMcpOperationResult,
   199	  };
   200	};
    85	
    86	/**
    87	 * Truncate long error messages to keep them readable
    88	 */
    89	const truncateErrorMessage = (message: string, maxLength: number = MCP_ERROR_MAX_LENGTH): string => {
    90	  if (message.length <= maxLength) {
    91	    return message;
    92	  }
    93	  return `${message.substring(0, Math.max(0, maxLength - 3))}...`;
    94	};
    95	
    96	const hasPublicationDivergence = (server: Pick<IMcpServer, 'lastError'>): boolean =>
    97	  typeof server.lastError === 'string' && server.lastError.includes(MCP_PUBLICATION_DIVERGENCE_MARKER);
    98	
    99	export function retainMcpPublicationReconciliation(
   100	  servers: IMcpServer[],
   101	  serverId: string,
   102	  fallback: IMcpServer
   103	): IMcpServer[] {
   104	  const exactIndex = servers.findIndex((candidate) => candidate.id === serverId);
   105	  const canonicalIndex = servers.findIndex(
   106	    (candidate) => mcpServerCollisionKey(candidate.name) === mcpServerCollisionKey(fallback.name)
   107	  );
   108	  const reconciliationIndex = exactIndex >= 0 ? exactIndex : canonicalIndex;
   109	  const current = reconciliationIndex >= 0 ? servers[reconciliationIndex] : undefined;
   110	  const reconciliationServer: IMcpServer = {
   111	    ...(current ?? fallback),
   112	    enabled: current?.enabled ?? false,
   113	    status: 'error',
   114	    lastError: MCP_PUBLICATION_DIVERGENCE_ERROR,
   115	    updatedAt: nextMcpRevision((current ?? fallback).updatedAt),
   116	  };
   117	
   118	  if (reconciliationIndex < 0) return [...servers, reconciliationServer];
   119	  const next = [...servers];
   120	  next[reconciliationIndex] = reconciliationServer;
   121	  return next;
   122	}
   123	
   124	const publicationDivergenceError = (probeError: string): string => {
   125	  const suffix = `; ${MCP_PUBLICATION_DIVERGENCE_MARKER} — reconnect this connector`;
   126	  return `${truncateErrorMessage(probeError, MCP_ERROR_MAX_LENGTH - suffix.length)}${suffix}`;
   127	};

    10	/**
    11	 * The 4-state UI status a server resolves to, shared by the Installed rows and
    12	 * the Browse cards so both surfaces describe a server's health identically.
    13	 *
    14	 * - reachable: enabled + standalone probe succeeded; chat readiness is unproven
    15	 * - warn:    needs the user to sign in again (OAuth expired / never completed)
    16	 * - error:   last connection attempt failed (see server.lastError for why)
    17	 * - stopped: disabled by the user, or enabled-but-not-yet-connected (idle)
    18	 */
    19	export type UIStatus = 'reachable' | 'warn' | 'error' | 'stopped';
    20	
    21	/**
    22	 * Derive the UI status from the stored server record + its live OAuth status.
    23	 * error short-circuits first, then warn (needsLogin), then reachable
    24	 * (enabled + probe-reachable), else stopped (absorbs disconnected / testing / undefined).
    25	 */
    26	export function deriveStatus(s: IMcpServer, oauth: McpOAuthStatus | undefined): UIStatus {
    27	  if (s.status === 'error') return 'error';
    28	  if (oauth?.needsLogin === true) return 'warn';
    29	  if (s.enabled === true && s.status === 'connected') return 'reachable';
    30	  return 'stopped';
    31	}
    32	
    33	/** A status counts as "needs the user's attention" when it is broken or wants a sign-in. */
    34	export function needsAttention(status: UIStatus): boolean {
    35	  return status === 'error' || status === 'warn';
    36	}

/**
 * @license
 * Copyright 2026 Ferrox Labs
 * SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
 */

import type { McpOperationResult } from '../McpProtocol';
import { AbstractMcpAgent } from '../McpProtocol';
import type { IMcpServer } from '@/common/config/storage';
import { ProcessConfig } from '@process/utils/initStorage';

/**
 * Wayland local MCP agent implementation
 *
 * Manages MCP configuration for the local Gemini CLI running via @office-ai/aioncli-core
 *
 * How it works:
 * 1. MCP configuration is stored in ProcessConfig under 'mcp.config'
 * 2. GeminiAgentManager reads from mcp.config at startup and converts it to @office-ai/aioncli-core format
 * 3. @office-ai/aioncli-core uses these MCP servers at runtime
 *
 * Differences vs. other ACP Backend MCP Agents:
 * - ACP Backend Agents: manage MCP configuration of real CLI tools (e.g. claude mcp, qwen mcp commands)
 * - WaylandMcpAgent: manages runtime MCP configuration for Wayland's local @office-ai/aioncli-core
 */
export class WaylandMcpAgent extends AbstractMcpAgent {
  constructor() {
    // Use 'wayland' as the backend type to distinguish from the real Gemini CLI
    // Even though config is consumed by GeminiAgentManager, at the MCP management layer it's a separate agent
    super('wayland');
  }

  getSupportedTransports(): string[] {
    // @office-ai/aioncli-core supports stdio, sse, http (streamable_http maps to http)
    // Reference: node_modules/@office-ai/aioncli-core/dist/src/config/config.d.ts -> MCPServerConfig
    return ['stdio', 'sse', 'http', 'streamable_http'];
  }

  /**
   * Detect MCP configuration managed by Wayland
   * Reads from the unified ProcessConfig
   */
  async detectMcpServers(_cliPath?: string): Promise<IMcpServer[]> {
    try {
      const mcpConfig = await ProcessConfig.get('mcp.config');
      if (!mcpConfig || !Array.isArray(mcpConfig)) {
        return [];
      }

      // Return all configured MCP servers
      // Filter to those with transport types supported by @office-ai/aioncli-core
      return mcpConfig.filter((server: IMcpServer) => {
        const supportedTypes = this.getSupportedTransports();
        return supportedTypes.includes(server.transport.type);
      });
    } catch (error) {
      console.warn('[WaylandMcpAgent] Failed to detect MCP servers:', error);
      return [];
    }
  }

  /**
   * Install MCP servers into the Wayland configuration
   * In practice, merges the entries into the unified ProcessConfig
   */
  async installMcpServers(mcpServers: IMcpServer[]): Promise<McpOperationResult> {
    try {
      // Read current configuration
      const currentConfig = (await ProcessConfig.get('mcp.config')) || [];
      const existingServers = Array.isArray(currentConfig) ? currentConfig : [];

      // Merge new servers (deduped by name)
      const serverMap = new Map<string, IMcpServer>();

      // Seed with existing servers first
      existingServers.forEach((server: IMcpServer) => {
        serverMap.set(server.name, server);
      });

      // Add or update incoming servers
      mcpServers.forEach((server) => {
        // Only install supported transport types
        if (this.getSupportedTransports().includes(server.transport.type)) {
          serverMap.set(server.name, {
            ...server,
            updatedAt: Date.now(),
          });
        } else {
          console.warn(`[WaylandMcpAgent] Skipping ${server.name}: unsupported transport type ${server.transport.type}`);
        }
      });

      // Convert back to an array and save
      const mergedServers = Array.from(serverMap.values());
      await ProcessConfig.set('mcp.config', mergedServers);

      console.log('[WaylandMcpAgent] Installed MCP servers:', mcpServers.map((s) => s.name).join(', '));
      return { success: true };
    } catch (error) {
      console.error('[WaylandMcpAgent] Failed to install MCP servers:', error);
      return { success: false, error: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error) };
    }
  }

  /**
   * Remove an MCP server from the Wayland configuration
   *
   * Note: Wayland's MCP configuration is managed centrally by the renderer (frontend).
   * This method is intentionally a no-op because:
   * 1. When toggled off: the frontend already sets enabled: false; no backend mutation needed
   * 2. When deleted: the frontend already removed it from config; no backend mutation needed
   *
   * WaylandMcpAgent is only responsible for reading config (detectMcpServers) and adding
   * config (installMcpServers). It should not mutate config during remove to avoid
   * conflicting with the frontend's configuration management.
   */
  removeMcpServer(mcpServerName: string): Promise<McpOperationResult> {
    console.log(`[WaylandMcpAgent] Skip removing '${mcpServerName}' - config managed by renderer`);
    return Promise.resolve({ success: true });
  }
}

 succeeded in 2797ms:
import type React from 'react';
import { useCallback } from 'react';
import { useTranslation } from 'react-i18next';
import { Message } from '@arco-design/web-react';
import { ConfigStorage } from '@/common/config/storage';
import type { IMcpServer } from '@/common/config/storage';

/**
 * MCP server CRUD operations hook.
 * Handles add/edit/delete and enable/disable for MCP servers.
 */
export const useMcpServerCRUD = (
  mcpServers: IMcpServer[],
  saveMcpServers: (serversOrUpdater: IMcpServer[] | ((prev: IMcpServer[]) => IMcpServer[])) => Promise<void>,
  syncMcpToAgents: (server: IMcpServer, skipRecheck?: boolean) => Promise<void>,
  removeMcpFromAgents: (serverName: string, successMessage?: string, transportType?: string) => Promise<void>,
  checkSingleServerInstallStatus: (serverName: string) => Promise<void>,
  setAgentInstallStatus: React.Dispatch<React.SetStateAction<Record<string, string[]>>>
) => {
  const { t } = useTranslation();

  // Add MCP server
  const handleAddMcpServer = useCallback(
    async (serverData: Omit<IMcpServer, 'id' | 'createdAt' | 'updatedAt'>) => {
      const now = Date.now();
      let serverToSync: IMcpServer | null = null;

      // Use functional update to avoid stale-closure issues
      await saveMcpServers((prevServers) => {
        const existingServerIndex = prevServers.findIndex((server) => server.name === serverData.name);

        if (existingServerIndex !== -1) {
          // If a server with the same name exists, update the existing one
          const updatedServers = [...prevServers];
          updatedServers[existingServerIndex] = {
            ...updatedServers[existingServerIndex],
            ...serverData,
            updatedAt: now,
          };
          serverToSync = updatedServers[existingServerIndex];
          return updatedServers;
        } else {
          // If no server with the same name exists, add a new server
          const newServer: IMcpServer = {
            ...serverData,
            id: `mcp_${now}`,
            createdAt: now,
            updatedAt: now,
          };
          serverToSync = newServer;
          return [...prevServers, newServer];
        }
      });

      // Check install status
      if (serverToSync) {
        setTimeout(() => void checkSingleServerInstallStatus(serverToSync.name), 100);
      }

      // Return the newly added/updated server for subsequent connection testing
      return serverToSync;
    },
    [saveMcpServers, syncMcpToAgents, t, checkSingleServerInstallStatus]
  );

  // Batch-import MCP servers
  const handleBatchImportMcpServers = useCallback(
    async (serversData: Omit<IMcpServer, 'id' | 'createdAt' | 'updatedAt'>[]) => {
      const now = Date.now();
      const addedServers: IMcpServer[] = [];

      // Use functional update to avoid stale-closure issues
      await saveMcpServers((prevServers) => {
        const updatedServers = [...prevServers];

        serversData.forEach((serverData, index) => {
          const existingServerIndex = updatedServers.findIndex((server) => server.name === serverData.name);

          if (existingServerIndex !== -1) {
            // If a server with the same name exists, update the existing one
            updatedServers[existingServerIndex] = {
              ...updatedServers[existingServerIndex],
              ...serverData,
              updatedAt: now,
            };
          } else {
            // If no server with the same name exists, add a new server
            const newServer: IMcpServer = {
              ...serverData,
              id: `mcp_${now}_${index}`,
              createdAt: now,
              updatedAt: now,
            };
            updatedServers.push(newServer);
            addedServers.push(newServer);
          }
        });

        return updatedServers;
      });

      // Check install status
      setTimeout(() => {
        serversData.forEach((serverData) => {
          void checkSingleServerInstallStatus(serverData.name);
        });
      }, 100);

      // Return list of newly added servers for subsequent connection testing
      return addedServers;
    },
    [saveMcpServers, syncMcpToAgents, t, checkSingleServerInstallStatus]
  );

  // Edit MCP server
  const handleEditMcpServer = useCallback(
    async (
      editingMcpServer: IMcpServer | undefined,
      serverData: Omit<IMcpServer, 'id' | 'createdAt' | 'updatedAt'>
    ): Promise<IMcpServer | undefined> => {
      if (!editingMcpServer) return undefined;

      let updatedServer: IMcpServer | undefined;

      // Use functional update to avoid stale-closure issues
      await saveMcpServers((prevServers) => {
        updatedServer = {
          ...editingMcpServer,
          ...serverData,
          updatedAt: Date.now(),
        };

        return prevServers.map((server) => (server.id === editingMcpServer.id ? updatedServer : server));
      });

      Message.success(t('settings.mcpImportSuccess'));
      // Immediately re-check install status for this server after editing (install status only)
      setTimeout(() => void checkSingleServerInstallStatus(serverData.name), 100);

      // Return the updated server object for subsequent connection testing
      return updatedServer;
    },
    [saveMcpServers, t, checkSingleServerInstallStatus]
  );

  // Delete MCP server
  const handleDeleteMcpServer = useCallback(
    async (serverId: string) => {
      let targetServer: IMcpServer | undefined;

      // Use functional update to avoid stale-closure issues
      await saveMcpServers((prevServers) => {
        targetServer = prevServers.find((server) => server.id === serverId);
        if (!targetServer) return prevServers;

        return prevServers.filter((server) => server.id !== serverId);
      });

      if (!targetServer) return;

      // After deletion, update install status directly without triggering detection
      setAgentInstallStatus((prev) => {
        const updated = { ...prev };
        delete updated[targetServer.name];
        // Also update local storage
        void ConfigStorage.set('mcp.agentInstallStatus', updated).catch(() => {
          // Handle storage error silently
        });
        return updated;
      });

      try {
        // If the server is enabled, remove the MCP config from all agents
        if (targetServer.enabled) {
          await removeMcpFromAgents(
            targetServer.name,
            t('settings.mcpDeletedWithCleanup'),
            targetServer.transport.type
          );
        } else {
          Message.success(t('settings.mcpDeleted'));
        }
      } catch (error) {
        Message.error(t('settings.mcpDeleteError'));
      }
    },
    [saveMcpServers, setAgentInstallStatus, removeMcpFromAgents, t]
  );

  // Enable/disable MCP server
  const handleToggleMcpServer = useCallback(
    async (serverId: string, enabled: boolean) => {
      let targetServer: IMcpServer | undefined;
      let updatedTargetServer: IMcpServer | undefined;

      // Use functional update to avoid stale-closure issues
      await saveMcpServers((prevServers) => {
        targetServer = prevServers.find((server) => server.id === serverId);
        if (!targetServer) return prevServers;

        return prevServers.map((server) => {
          if (server.id === serverId) {
            updatedTargetServer = { ...server, enabled, updatedAt: Date.now() };
            return updatedTargetServer;
          }
          return server;
        });
      });

      if (!targetServer || !updatedTargetServer) return;

      try {
        if (enabled) {
          // If the MCP server is enabled, sync only the current server to all detected agents
          await syncMcpToAgents(updatedTargetServer, true);
          // Immediately re-check install status after enabling (install status only)
          setTimeout(() => void checkSingleServerInstallStatus(targetServer.name), 100);
        } else {
          // If the MCP server is disabled, remove the config from all agents
          await removeMcpFromAgents(targetServer.name, undefined, targetServer.transport.type);
          // After disabling, update UI state directly; no re-detection needed
          setAgentInstallStatus((prev) => {
            const updated = { ...prev };
            delete updated[targetServer.name];
            // Also update local storage
            void ConfigStorage.set('mcp.agentInstallStatus', updated).catch(() => {
              // Handle storage error silently
            });
            return updated;
          });
        }
      } catch (error) {
        Message.error(enabled ? t('settings.mcpSyncError') : t('settings.mcpRemoveError'));
      }
    },
    [saveMcpServers, syncMcpToAgents, removeMcpFromAgents, checkSingleServerInstallStatus, setAgentInstallStatus, t]
  );

  return {
    handleAddMcpServer,
    handleBatchImportMcpServers,
    handleEditMcpServer,
    handleDeleteMcpServer,
    handleToggleMcpServer,
  };
};

   142	export interface UseMcpLibrary {
   143	  entries: CatalogIndexEntry[];
   144	  recommended: CatalogIndexEntry[];
   145	  byTier: Record<Tier, CatalogIndexEntry[]>;
   146	  byCategory: Record<string, CatalogIndexEntry[]>;

   150	
   151	/**
   152	 * Hand-curated "Recommended for you" connectors, in deliberate order. These are
   153	 * the mainstream services most people actually reach for. We pick them by id
   154	 * rather than sorting on `popularityRank` because that float drifted
   155	 * platform-specific niches (Apple - macOS only) into the top slots. Apple and
   156	 * other OS-bound connectors stay fully browsable, just not "recommended".
   157	 */
   158	const RECOMMENDED_IDS = [
   159	  'io.github.taylorwilsdon/google-workspace-mcp', // Google Workspace
   160	  'com.slack/slack-mcp', // Slack
   161	  'com.github/github-mcp-server', // GitHub
   162	  'com.notion/notion-mcp', // Notion
   163	  'com.linear/linear-mcp', // Linear
   164	  'com.softeria/ms-365-mcp-server', // Microsoft 365
   165	];
   166	const RECOMMENDED_COUNT = 6;
   167	
   168	export function useMcpLibrary(): UseMcpLibrary {
   169	  return useMemo(() => {
   170	    const idx = catalog as unknown as CatalogIndex;
   171	    const entries = idx.entries
   172	      .toSorted((a, b) => a.popularityRank - b.popularityRank)
   173	      .map((e) => Object.assign({}, e, { iconUrl: resolveIconUrl(e.iconUrl) ?? e.iconUrl }));
   174	    // Build the recommended row from the curated id list, falling back to
   175	    // rank order for any id that isn't found so the row is always full.
   176	    const byId = new Map(entries.map((e) => [e.id, e]));
   177	    const recommended = RECOMMENDED_IDS.map((id) => byId.get(id)).filter(
   178	      (e): e is CatalogIndexEntry => e !== undefined,
   179	    );
   180	    for (const e of entries) {
   181	      if (recommended.length >= RECOMMENDED_COUNT) break;
   182	      if (!recommended.includes(e)) recommended.push(e);
   183	    }
   184	    const byTier: Record<Tier, CatalogIndexEntry[]> = { core: [], worker: [], builder: [] };
   185	    const byCategory: Record<string, CatalogIndexEntry[]> = {};
   186	    for (const e of entries) {
   187	      byTier[e.tier].push(e);
   188	      for (const c of e.categories) (byCategory[c] ??= []).push(e);
   189	    }
   190	    return {
    80	     * URL query parameter the api-key token is appended to for a hosted
    81	     * (remote) server that authenticates via the URL rather than a header -
    82	     * e.g. Exa's `https://mcp.exa.ai/mcp?exaApiKey=<token>`. Mutually exclusive
    83	     * with `header`: when set, the token is injected into the remote URL and no
    84	     * Authorization header is sent (header auth is silently ignored by these
    85	     * servers, which is why the stdio fallback appeared to "not save the key").
    86	     */
    87	    queryParam?: string;
    88	    scopes?: { name: string; plainLanguage: string }[];
    89	    /**
    90	     * Vendor hint for the BYO-credentials flow. When the OAuth server does
    91	     * not support Dynamic Client Registration (Slack, GitHub, HubSpot,
    92	     * Zoom, Box, Figma...), the user must register an OAuth app on the
    93	     * vendor's developer console and paste the client_id/secret here.
    94	     * Absent on vendors that support DCR (Notion, Linear, Stripe, ...) -
    95	     * for those, the universal BYO modal handles unexpected DCR failures.
    96	     */
    97	    byoClient?: {
    98	      /** URL of the vendor's "create an OAuth app" page. */
    99	      registrationUrl: string;
   100	      /** Vendor-specific markdown shown above the credential inputs. */
   101	      guide?: string;
   102	      /**
   103	       * Redirect URI the user must paste into the vendor console.
   104	       * Defaults to http://localhost:57000/oauth/callback (Wayland's pinned
   105	       * OAUTH_CALLBACK_PORT). Override if the vendor enforces something
   106	       * specific (e.g. https-only).
   107	       */
   108	      redirectUriHint?: string;
   109	      /** Whether this vendor requires both client_id AND client_secret. */
   110	      requiresSecret?: boolean;
   111	    };
   112	  };
   113	  toolGroups?: { label: string; count: number }[];
   114	  setupGuide?: { path: string; estimatedMinutes: number; stepCount: number };
   115	  /**
   116	   * Agent-facing usage guidance injected into the system prompt when this
   117	   * connector is enabled (#475). Use for tools whose own descriptions don't
   118	   * spell out required arguments — e.g. the Google Workspace MCP's
   119	   * `start_google_auth`, which requires `service_name`. Keep it terse; it is
   120	   * added to every turn while the connector is on.
   121	   */
   122	  agentGuidance?: string;
   123	  platforms?: ('macos' | 'windows' | 'linux')[];
   124	  minWaylandVersion?: string;

   761	              ? t('mcpLibrary.detail.notConnectedOauth', 'Installed. Sign in to connect it.')
   762	              : t('mcpLibrary.detail.notConnectedKey', 'Installed. Add your token to connect it.')}
   763	          </div>
   764	          <button
   765	            type='button'
   766	            className={`${styles.btnPrimary} ${styles.btnWarn}`}
   767	            onClick={() => (isOauth ? void onPrimary('oauth-flow') : setTab('setup-guide'))}
   768	            disabled={oauthInFlight}
   769	          >
   770	            <LogIn size={16} />
   771	            {isOauth
   772	              ? oauthInFlight
   773	                ? t('mcpLibrary.detail.signingIn', 'Signing in…')
   774	                : t('mcpLibrary.detail.signIn', 'Sign in')
   775	              : t('mcpLibrary.detail.addToken', 'Add token')}
   776	          </button>
   777	          {isOauth && oauthInFlight && installedServer && (
   778	            <button type='button' className={styles.btn2} onClick={() => cancelOAuth(installedServer)}>
   779	              {t('mcpLibrary.detail.cancelSignIn', 'Cancel sign-in')}
   780	            </button>
   781	          )}
   782	          <div className={styles.lifecycle}>
   783	            <button type='button' className={`${styles.btn2} ${styles.btn2Danger}`} onClick={confirmRemove}>
   784	              <Trash2 size={14} />
   785	              {t('mcpLibrary.detail.remove', 'Remove connector')}
   786	            </button>
   787	          </div>
   788	        </div>
   789	      );
   790	    }
   791	
   792	    // STATE 2: Stored, probed, or published. No wording here claims the active
   793	    // chat has registered the tools; only a session receipt may do that.
   794	    return (
   795	      <div className={`${styles.action} ${disabled ? '' : styles.actionConnected}`}>
   796	        {disabled ? (
   797	          <div className={styles.statusLine}>
   798	            <span className={`${styles.dot} ${styles.dotOff}`} />
   799	            {t('mcpLibrary.detail.off', 'Off')}
   800	          </div>
   801	        ) : uiStatus === 'reachable' ? (
   802	          <StatusChip status='reachable' />
   803	        ) : (
   804	          <div className={styles.statusLine}>
   805	            <span className={`${styles.dot} ${styles.dotOff}`} />
   806	            {t('mcpLibrary.detail.enabledUnverified', 'Enabled · chat not verified')}
   807	          </div>
   808	        )}
   809	        <div className={styles.statusMeta} style={{ marginTop: 8 }}>
   810	          {uiStatus === 'reachable'
   811	            ? t('mcpLibrary.detail.reachableMeta', '{{count}} tools reported by the server probe', { count: toolCount })
   812	            : t('mcpLibrary.detail.publishedMeta', 'Published configuration; open a new chat to verify its tools.')}
   813	          {syncedAt ? ` · ${t('mcpLibrary.detail.lastSynced', 'synced {{time}}', { time: syncedAt })}` : ''}
   814	        </div>
   815	        <div className={styles.ctrlRow}>
   816	          <span className={styles.ctrlKey}>{t('mcpLibrary.detail.enabled', 'Enabled')}</span>
   817	          <Switch
   818	            checked={installedServer.enabled === true}
   819	            onChange={(v) => void crud.handleToggleMcpServer(installedServer.id, v)}
   820	          />
   821	        </div>
   822	        <div className={styles.lifecycle}>
   823	          <button type='button' className={styles.btn2} onClick={reconnect} disabled={reconnecting}>

   833	            {t('mcpLibrary.detail.remove', 'Remove connector')}
   834	          </button>
   835	        </div>

   838	          {t('mcpLibrary.detail.lifecycleNote', 'Disable keeps your sign-in. Remove deletes it.')}
   839	        </div>

   187	    [saveMcpServers, setAgentInstallStatus, removeMcpFromAgents, t]

   189	
   190	  // Enable/disable MCP server
   191	  const handleToggleMcpServer = useCallback(
   192	    async (serverId: string, enabled: boolean) => {
   193	      let targetServer: IMcpServer | undefined;
   194	      let updatedTargetServer: IMcpServer | undefined;
   195	
   196	      // Use functional update to avoid stale-closure issues
   197	      await saveMcpServers((prevServers) => {
   198	        targetServer = prevServers.find((server) => server.id === serverId);
   199	        if (!targetServer) return prevServers;
   200	
   201	        return prevServers.map((server) => {
   202	          if (server.id === serverId) {
   203	            updatedTargetServer = { ...server, enabled, updatedAt: Date.now() };
   204	            return updatedTargetServer;
   205	          }
   206	          return server;
   207	        });
   208	      });
   209	
   210	      if (!targetServer || !updatedTargetServer) return;
   211	
   212	      try {
   213	        if (enabled) {
   214	          // If the MCP server is enabled, sync only the current server to all detected agents
   215	          await syncMcpToAgents(updatedTargetServer, true);
   216	          // Immediately re-check install status after enabling (install status only)
   217	          setTimeout(() => void checkSingleServerInstallStatus(targetServer.name), 100);
   218	        } else {
   219	          // If the MCP server is disabled, remove the config from all agents
   220	          await removeMcpFromAgents(targetServer.name, undefined, targetServer.transport.type);
   221	          // After disabling, update UI state directly; no re-detection needed
   222	          setAgentInstallStatus((prev) => {
   223	            const updated = { ...prev };
   224	            delete updated[targetServer.name];
   225	            // Also update local storage
   226	            void ConfigStorage.set('mcp.agentInstallStatus', updated).catch(() => {
   227	              // Handle storage error silently
   228	            });
   229	            return updated;
   230	          });
   231	        }
   232	      } catch (error) {
   233	        Message.error(enabled ? t('settings.mcpSyncError') : t('settings.mcpRemoveError'));
   234	      }
   235	    },

codex
VERDICT: FIX-FIRST

1. **SEVERITY: critical — The root cause is determinable: H1 is triggered by `WaylandMcpAgent` writing the row during publication.**

   **Evidence:** `handleToggleMcpServer` constructs an enabled declaration and sends it to the agents before committing locally (`src/renderer/hooks/mcp/useMcpServerCRUD.ts:355-370`). Fork Gemini is routed to `WaylandMcpAgent` (`src/process/services/mcpServices/McpService.ts:113-117`), which writes that enabled declaration directly into the authoritative `mcp.config` and increments `updatedAt` (`src/process/services/mcpServices/agents/WaylandMcpAgent.ts:67-87`). When publication finishes, the renderer compares against the original disabled row revision and rejects it (`src/renderer/hooks/mcp/useMcpServerCRUD.ts:377-385`).

   H2 is not the observed failure. The Wayland write completes before `syncMcpToAgents` resolves; the subsequent renderer save reads the new whole-config revision, so its global CAS can apply successfully. Its updater merely returns the row unchanged because `updatedAt` differs, leaving `committed === false`.

   **Correction:** Delete the H1/H2 investigation fork. Name the cause as self-publication through `WaylandMcpAgent`. Make the renderer/main config authority the sole writer of Wayland’s local declaration: either exclude `WaylandMcpAgent` from pre-commit external publication or make its install operation validation-only/no-op. Do not weaken the row CAS to accommodate this unauthorized writer.

2. **SEVERITY: high — The claimed “complete” 16-writer inventory is incomplete, and none of those 16 explains the deterministic reproduction.**

   **Evidence:** The list covers only renderer `saveMcpServers` calls. It omits direct main-process writers such as `WaylandMcpAgent` above and BYO credential persistence (`src/process/bridge/mcpBridge.ts:65-75`).

   Of the 16 renderer sites, ten can change this row only if independently invoked: CRUD add/import/edit/toggle/reconciliation (`useMcpServerCRUD.ts:74`, `:174`, `:275`, `:377`, `:419`), direct-probe reconciliation (`useMcpConnection.ts:165`, `:287`, `:303`), catalog probe persistence and tool scoping (`DetailPage.tsx:318`, `:619`). The observation-only update at `useMcpConnection.ts:206` returns the array unchanged; passive refresh excludes a durably disabled target (`useMcpConnection.ts:449-454`); the four image-generation writers target only the built-in image server (`ToolsModalContent.tsx:859`, `:927`; `ImageGenSettings/index.tsx:114`, `:162`).

   **Correction:** Inventory every `ProcessConfig.set/update`, `updateMcpConfig`, archive/restore operation, migration writer, and renderer CAS—not merely `saveMcpServers`.

3. **SEVERITY: high — Phase A cannot name the writer as written.**

   **Evidence:** Logging `enqueueMcpConfigWrite` observes only renderer writes (`src/renderer/hooks/mcp/useMcpServers.ts:35-60`). The causal Wayland write bypasses that queue through `updateMcpConfig`. Phase A would reveal the H1 exception and an already-changed row, but not which operation changed it. Comparing only `updatedAt` also misses that publication passes a normalized, sanitized, potentially OAuth-token-bearing declaration (`McpService.ts:296-300`, `:327-350`) and `WaylandMcpAgent` persists the whole object.

   The queue’s `.catch(() => undefined)` is not swallowing the caller’s error: it is assigned only to the queue tail while the original `operation` is returned (`useMcpServers.ts:59-60`). `publishMcpConfig` also runs only after confirmed persistence (`useMcpServers.ts:45-55`). Neither is the root cause.

   **Correction:** Instrument the main-process config authority with transaction ID, caller/authority, before/after revision, complete row diff, and adapter identity. Log the original error, `cause`, adapter results, and rollback errors at the toggle boundary.

4. **SEVERITY: critical — “Keep it and re-commit” is not a valid general recovery rule.**

   **Evidence:** Publication-first ordering deliberately prevents a failed or partial publication from leaving a locally enabled false-green row (`useMcpServerCRUD.ts:365-370`). The row guard prevents a stale 45-second transaction from overwriting a concurrent edit or resurrecting a deleted connector (`useMcpServerCRUD.ts:377-385`). Reconciliation deliberately preserves the current winner instead of the stale publication candidate (`useMcpConnection.ts:99-121`).

   **Correction:** Treat a local commit as already satisfied only when current durable truth is materially identical to the exact publication candidate. A concurrent edit, disable, rename, or deletion must win; adapters must then be reconciled to that winner. Fix the self-writing Wayland adapter instead of broadly retrying stale commits.

5. **SEVERITY: critical — The plan ignores a second invariant violation inside `retainMcpPublicationReconciliation`.**

   **Evidence:** `WaylandMcpAgent` writes the enabled row, but its removal operation is explicitly a no-op (`WaylandMcpAgent.ts:97-111`). If another adapter fails and compensation otherwise succeeds, `handleToggleMcpServer` returns `false` without restoring local disabled truth (`useMcpServerCRUD.ts:403-432`). If compensation fails, `retainMcpPublicationReconciliation` preserves `current.enabled`, which is now `true` because of the Wayland self-write (`useMcpConnection.ts:109-115`).

   Thus the system can retain either an enabled non-divergence row after “successful” compensation or an enabled divergence row after failed compensation.

   **Correction:** Add explicit tests for full publication success, partial publication, complete rollback, incomplete rollback, restart, retry, edit, and deletion. Retention must derive enabled truth from uncontaminated pre-transaction authority, not from a row already mutated by an adapter.

6. **SEVERITY: high — The W-1 acceptance test encodes the wrong behavior and the wrong baseline.**

   **Evidence:** “A slow sync with a competing writer must still commit” would require overwriting legitimate same-row edits. Duration is not causal; the Wayland self-write is. Also, current `main` commits locally before publication (`main:src/renderer/hooks/mcp/useMcpServerCRUD.ts:197-215`), so this specific publication-before-CAS regression test will not fail there as demanded by the plan.

   **Correction:** Require separate falsifiable cases:

   - Wayland publication must not mutate `mcp.config` before the renderer commit.
   - Unrelated-row churn must not prevent the commit.
   - An identical already-committed candidate may be accepted idempotently.
   - A material same-row edit or deletion must win and must not be overwritten.
   - Partial adapter publication must leave retryable, fail-closed durable truth.

   Pin the test to the pre-fix packet branch commit, not `main`.

7. **SEVERITY: high — W-2 is parallelizable but not independent of the integrated acceptance gate.**

   **Evidence:** Any failed adapter result causes `syncMcpToAgents` to reject before the local commit (`src/renderer/hooks/mcp/useMcpOperations.ts:169-181`). Claude stdio replacement and absent-removal handling directly affect reconnect and compensation (`ClaudeMcpAgent.ts:216-248`, `:300-348`).

   **Correction:** Implement W-1 and W-2 in parallel, but require both before end-to-end W-1 acceptance and before W-3 ships. W-3’s release gate must include idempotent Claude republish and rollback.

8. **SEVERITY: high — Catalog installation does reach `handleToggleMcpServer`, but only after additional untested stages.**

   **Evidence:** For `local-credentials`, the Install button routes to `oauth-flow` (`DetailPage.tsx:649-670`); stdio then routes to `saveAndConnect` (`DetailPage.tsx:385-394`). That path first persists a disabled row, probes it, writes probe status and a new `updatedAt`, and only then toggles/publishes (`DetailPage.tsx:288-340`).

   **Correction:** Add a full catalog-install test covering entry conversion → disabled persistence → bundled-Bun probe → probe write → toggle → five-agent publication → restart. Also test the TradingView-not-running path and successful retry after the prerequisite is satisfied. A CRUD-only reconnect test does not validate W-3.

9. **SEVERITY: medium — Several source claims are factually wrong.**

   **Evidence:** Agent publication is concurrent via `Promise.all`, not serialized (`McpService.ts:336-365`). The connector does not remain disabled during publication because Wayland writes it enabled (`WaylandMcpAgent.ts:69-79`). Rollback cannot “strip it from every agent” because Wayland removal is a no-op (`WaylandMcpAgent.ts:97-111`). The UI is not literally without recovery: its error state renders a Reconnect action (`DetailPage.tsx:721-737`); the action merely loops because the underlying bug repeats.

   **Correction:** Remove those causal claims. Describe the state as “operationally stuck in a deterministic reconnect loop,” not as lacking a UI recovery action.

10. **SEVERITY: medium — W-3 declares platform compatibility that the catalog does not enforce.**

   **Evidence:** `platforms` and `minWaylandVersion` exist as schema metadata (`src/renderer/mcp-catalog/schema/entry.schema.json:57-122`), but library construction loads and exposes every entry without platform or version filtering (`useMcpLibrary.ts:168-188`). The plan’s statement that `x-wayland` “supports only platforms and minWaylandVersion” is also false as written; the same schema defines tier, categories, tags, auth, setup guide, tool groups, and agent guidance.

   **Correction:** Define the Linux behavior explicitly: either implement platform gating, disable installation with an explanation, or document that `platforms` is informational and test that behavior. Do not claim the metadata is enforced.

   **Evidence:** Any failed adapter result causes `syncMcpToAgents` to reject before the local commit (`src/renderer/hooks/mcp/useMcpOperations.ts:169-181`). Claude stdio replacement and absent-removal handling directly affect reconnect and compensation (`ClaudeMcpAgent.ts:216-248`, `:300-348`).

   **Correction:** Implement W-1 and W-2 in parallel, but require both before end-to-end W-1 acceptance and before W-3 ships. W-3’s release gate must include idempotent Claude republish and rollback.

8. **SEVERITY: high — Catalog installation does reach `handleToggleMcpServer`, but only after additional untested stages.**

   **Evidence:** For `local-credentials`, the Install button routes to `oauth-flow` (`DetailPage.tsx:649-670`); stdio then routes to `saveAndConnect` (`DetailPage.tsx:385-394`). That path first persists a disabled row, probes it, writes probe status and a new `updatedAt`, and only then toggles/publishes (`DetailPage.tsx:288-340`).

   **Correction:** Add a full catalog-install test covering entry conversion → disabled persistence → bundled-Bun probe → probe write → toggle → five-agent publication → restart. Also test the TradingView-not-running path and successful retry after the prerequisite is satisfied. A CRUD-only reconnect test does not validate W-3.

9. **SEVERITY: medium — Several source claims are factually wrong.**

   **Evidence:** Agent publication is concurrent via `Promise.all`, not serialized (`McpService.ts:336-365`). The connector does not remain disabled during publication because Wayland writes it enabled (`WaylandMcpAgent.ts:69-79`). Rollback cannot “strip it from every agent” because Wayland removal is a no-op (`WaylandMcpAgent.ts:97-111`). The UI is not literally without recovery: its error state renders a Reconnect action (`DetailPage.tsx:721-737`); the action merely loops because the underlying bug repeats.

   **Correction:** Remove those causal claims. Describe the state as “operationally stuck in a deterministic reconnect loop,” not as lacking a UI recovery action.

10. **SEVERITY: medium — W-3 declares platform compatibility that the catalog does not enforce.**

   **Evidence:** `platforms` and `minWaylandVersion` exist as schema metadata (`src/renderer/mcp-catalog/schema/entry.schema.json:57-122`), but library construction loads and exposes every entry without platform or version filtering (`useMcpLibrary.ts:168-188`). The plan’s statement that `x-wayland` “supports only platforms and minWaylandVersion” is also false as written; the same schema defines tier, categories, tags, auth, setup guide, tool groups, and agent guidance.

   **Correction:** Define the Linux behavior explicitly: either implement platform gating, disable installation with an explanation, or document that `platforms` is informational and test that behavior. Do not claim the metadata is enforced.
